CSC Mastery — Answer Key Backup

Verbal Ability · Sentence Structure

Backup file only. This file stores correct answers, explanations, and quick lessons. It does not change the live question bank or website.

Sentence Structure — Questions 1–50

Question 1 C

Correct answer: She doesn't know the answer.

Explanation: “Doesn't” already carries the present-tense marker, so the main verb stays in its base form, “know.”

Quick lesson: After does/doesn't, use the base verb: doesn't know, doesn't go.

Question 2 B

Correct answer: The students were preparing for the examination.

Explanation: “Students” is plural, so the past form of “be” is “were.”

Quick lesson: Use was with singular subjects and were with plural subjects.

Question 3 A

Correct answer: Because it was raining, the event was postponed.

Explanation: “Because” already introduces the reason. Adding “so” creates an unnecessary double connector.

Quick lesson: Use either “Because ..., ...” or “..., so ...”; not both together.

Question 4 D

Correct answer: He is interested in learning new skills.

Explanation: The correct expression is “interested in,” followed by a noun or an -ing verb.

Quick lesson: Remember: interested in learning, interested in music.

Question 5 B

Correct answer: Neither the teacher nor the students were ready.

Explanation: With “neither ... nor,” the verb commonly agrees with the nearer subject. “Students” is plural.

Quick lesson: When subjects are joined by neither/nor, check the subject closest to the verb.

Question 6 A

Correct answer: She enjoys reading, writing, and painting.

Explanation: All three activities use the same -ing form, making the list parallel.

Quick lesson: Keep items in a list in the same grammatical form.

Question 7 C

Correct answer: The manager, along with his employees, is attending the meeting.

Explanation: The main subject is “manager.” The phrase “along with his employees” does not make it plural.

Quick lesson: Phrases such as along with, as well as, and together with do not change the main subject.

Question 8 B

Correct answer: I do not know where the office is.

Explanation: An indirect question uses normal statement word order: subject before verb.

Quick lesson: Direct: Where is the office? Indirect: I know where the office is.

Question 9 A

Correct answer: Every applicant must submit their form before Friday.

Explanation: “Every applicant” is singular, and singular “they/their” is accepted in modern standard English. “Must” takes the base verb.

Quick lesson: Use must + base verb. Singular they/their is acceptable when gender is unknown.

Question 10 A

Correct answer: The report was completed by the employee yesterday.

Explanation: “Report” is singular, so the passive construction uses “was completed.”

Quick lesson: Past passive: was/were + past participle.

Question 11 C

Correct answer: Although he was tired, he continued working.

Explanation: “Although” already shows contrast, so “but” or “so” is not needed.

Quick lesson: Use although + clause without adding but in the same construction.

Question 12 B

Correct answer: The teacher explained the lesson to us.

Explanation: The standard pattern is “explain something to someone.”

Quick lesson: Say “explain the lesson to us,” not “explain us the lesson.”

Question 13 D

Correct answer: My brother is taller than I am.

Explanation: “Taller” is already the comparative form. “More taller” and “most taller” are incorrect double comparisons.

Quick lesson: Use taller, not more taller.

Question 14 A

Correct answer: The employees who work hard usually achieve their goals.

Explanation: “Who” refers to the plural noun “employees,” so the verb is “work.”

Quick lesson: A relative-clause verb agrees with the noun that “who” refers to.

Question 15 C

Correct answer: She has gone to the office.

Explanation: The present perfect uses has/have + past participle. The past participle of “go” is “gone.”

Quick lesson: Present perfect: has/have + past participle.

Question 16 B

Correct answer: The reason for his absence was that he was sick.

Explanation: This is a clear, grammatically correct way to state the reason without the awkward “reason ... is because” construction.

Quick lesson: Use “The reason was that ...” or “The reason for ... was that ...”.

Question 17 C

Correct answer: If I were you, I would accept the offer.

Explanation: “Were” is the standard subjunctive form used in this unreal or hypothetical expression.

Quick lesson: For advice in a hypothetical situation, use “If I were you ...”.

Question 18 A

Correct answer: The office needs employees who are honest, responsible, and hardworking.

Explanation: The three words are all adjectives, so the list is parallel.

Quick lesson: Use the same part of speech for items in a series.

Question 19 D

Correct answer: He asked me whether I was ready.

Explanation: An indirect yes/no question uses “whether” or “if” followed by statement word order.

Quick lesson: Use whether/if + subject + verb in an indirect question.

Question 20 B

Correct answer: The book, together with the notebooks, belongs to her.

Explanation: The main subject is the singular “book.” “Together with the notebooks” is extra information.

Quick lesson: Ignore interrupting phrases when deciding subject-verb agreement.

Question 21 A

Correct answer: She speaks English more fluently than her brother.

Explanation: “Fluently” is an adverb describing how she speaks. The comparative form is “more fluently.”

Quick lesson: Use an adverb to describe a verb: speaks fluently.

Question 22 C

Correct answer: Before the meeting started, everyone had taken their seats.

Explanation: The past perfect uses “had” plus the past participle “taken.”

Quick lesson: Past perfect: had + past participle.

Question 23 B

Correct answer: The supervisor told the workers to complete the task.

Explanation: The pattern is “tell + person + to + base verb.”

Quick lesson: Use told someone to do something.

Question 24 D

Correct answer: There are fewer applicants this year than last year.

Explanation: “Applicants” is a countable plural noun, so “fewer” is the correct choice.

Quick lesson: Use fewer for countable things and less for uncountable amounts.

Question 25 B

Correct answer: Despite the heavy rain, the workers continued their duties.

Explanation: “Despite” is followed by a noun phrase, not “of” or a complete clause.

Quick lesson: Use despite + noun/-ing phrase; use although + subject + verb.

Question 26 B

Correct answer: The students have finished their assignments.

Explanation: “Students” is plural, so we use “have,” not “has.”

Quick lesson: Use have with plural subjects and has with singular subjects.

Question 27 C

Correct answer: She is good at solving mathematical problems.

Explanation: The correct expression is “good at” followed by a noun or an -ing verb.

Quick lesson: Remember: good at singing, writing, or solving problems.

Question 28 A

Correct answer: The manager gave the employees clear instructions.

Explanation: This follows the correct structure: subject + verb + indirect object + direct object.

Quick lesson: “Give someone something” is a correct sentence pattern.

Question 29 A

Correct answer: He works hard because he wants to succeed.

Explanation: “Because” introduces a complete clause. “Because of” is followed by a noun or noun phrase.

Quick lesson: Use because + subject + verb; use because of + noun.

Question 30 B

Correct answer: The teacher asked whether the students understood the lesson.

Explanation: Indirect questions use normal statement word order. We do not put “did” before the subject.

Quick lesson: Direct: Did they understand? Indirect: whether they understood.

Question 31 C

Correct answer: Neither of the answers is correct.

Explanation: In formal standard English, “neither” is treated as singular, so it takes “is.”

Quick lesson: For formal exam grammar, use neither + singular verb.

Question 32 C

Correct answer: She prefers coffee to tea.

Explanation: The standard expression is “prefer X to Y.”

Quick lesson: Use prefer + noun + to + noun.

Question 33 A

Correct answer: The students were excited about joining the competition.

Explanation: “Students” is plural, so we use “were.” “Excited about” is followed by an -ing verb.

Quick lesson: Use excited about doing something.

Question 34 C

Correct answer: He is the smartest student in the class.

Explanation: “Smartest” is already the superlative form. Adding “most” is unnecessary.

Quick lesson: Do not use most with adjectives that already end in -est.

Question 35 A

Correct answer: The officer explained the procedure to the applicants.

Explanation: The correct structure is “explain something to someone.”

Quick lesson: Say “explain the rules to us,” not “explain us the rules.”

Question 36 A

Correct answer: If she studies regularly, she will improve her scores.

Explanation: This is the first conditional: present simple in the if-clause and “will” in the result clause.

Quick lesson: First conditional: If + present simple, will + base verb.

Question 37 A

Correct answer: The documents were carefully checked by the clerk.

Explanation: “Documents” is plural, so the passive sentence uses “were.” “Carefully” describes how they were checked.

Quick lesson: Passive voice: subject + be verb + past participle.

Question 38 B

Correct answer: He is responsible for preparing the report.

Explanation: The correct expression is “responsible for,” followed by a noun or an -ing verb.

Quick lesson: Remember: responsible for doing something.

Question 39 C

Correct answer: The woman who works here is my aunt.

Explanation: “Who” refers to a person and acts as the subject of “works.”

Quick lesson: Use who for people, which for things, and whose for possession.

Question 40 B

Correct answer: The manager said the office would close early.

Explanation: “Said” can introduce a statement without naming the listener. “Told” usually needs an object, such as “told us.”

Quick lesson: Say something; tell someone something.

Question 41 B

Correct answer: He has been working here for five years.

Explanation: “For five years” describes a period of time. We use “for,” not “since,” for durations.

Quick lesson: Use for with a length of time and since with a starting point.

Question 42 B

Correct answer: The students not only studied hard but also practiced daily.

Explanation: The two parts are parallel: “studied” and “practiced” are both past-tense verbs.

Quick lesson: Keep the grammatical structure balanced after not only ... but also.

Question 43 C

Correct answer: The number of applicants is increasing every year.

Explanation: The subject is “the number,” which is singular. “Applicants” does not control the verb.

Quick lesson: Use the number of + plural noun + singular verb.

Question 44 A

Correct answer: She asked me where I was going.

Explanation: Indirect questions use statement word order: subject first, followed by the verb.

Quick lesson: Say “where I was going,” not “where was I going,” in an indirect question.

Question 45 B

Correct answer: The children enjoyed playing in the park.

Explanation: The verb “enjoy” is followed by a gerund, or an -ing verb.

Quick lesson: Use enjoy + verb-ing, such as enjoy reading or enjoy swimming.

Question 46 C

Correct answer: Despite being tired, he continued working.

Explanation: “Despite” can be followed by a noun or an -ing phrase. It is not followed directly by a complete clause.

Quick lesson: Use despite + noun/gerund; use although + subject + verb.

Question 47 D

Correct answer: The employees must follow the company rules.

Explanation: Modal verbs like “must” are followed by the base form of the verb.

Quick lesson: Use must + base verb: must go, must study, must follow.

Question 48 A

Correct answer: The book is too expensive for me to buy.

Explanation: This uses the correct pattern “too + adjective + for someone + to + verb.”

Quick lesson: Remember: too difficult to understand; too expensive to buy.

Question 49 B

Correct answer: She is one of the students who work hard.

Explanation: “Who” refers to “students,” which is plural. Therefore, the verb is “work.”

Quick lesson: In “one of the people who ...,” the verb after who normally agrees with people.

Question 50 C

Correct answer: The meeting was cancelled because the manager was absent.

Explanation: “Because” is followed by a complete clause: “the manager was absent.” “Because of” must be followed by a noun phrase.

Quick lesson: Use because + subject + verb; use because of + noun.

Sentence Structure — Questions 51–75

Question 51 B

Correct answer: The applicants have completed their registration.

Explanation: “Applicants” is plural, so the present perfect uses “have,” not “has.”

Quick lesson: Use have with plural subjects and has with singular subjects.

Question 52 C

Correct answer: She has been working at the company since 2020.

Explanation: “Since 2020” gives the starting point of an action that continues to the present. The present perfect continuous fits this meaning.

Quick lesson: Use since + starting point; use for + duration.

Question 53 A

Correct answer: The teacher encouraged the students to study harder.

Explanation: The correct pattern is “encourage someone to do something.”

Quick lesson: Use encourage + person + to + base verb.

Question 54 D

Correct answer: He is taller than his brother.

Explanation: “Taller” is already the comparative form, and comparisons use “than.”

Quick lesson: Use adjective + -er + than, or more + adjective + than.

Question 55 B

Correct answer: The report, along with the attachments, was submitted yesterday.

Explanation: The main subject is the singular “report.” The phrase “along with the attachments” does not change the subject.

Quick lesson: Ignore phrases such as along with when choosing the verb.

Question 56 C

Correct answer: I look forward to meeting you next week.

Explanation: In “look forward to,” the word “to” is a preposition, so it is followed by a noun or gerund.

Quick lesson: Use look forward to + verb-ing.

Question 57 A

Correct answer: The students were asked to bring their notebooks and pencils.

Explanation: “Students” is plural, so the passive form uses “were.” The structure “asked to bring” is correct.

Quick lesson: Passive: were + past participle; asked + to + base verb.

Question 58 D

Correct answer: He did not know the answer.

Explanation: After “did not,” use the base form “know,” not the past form “knew.”

Quick lesson: Use did not + base verb.

Question 59 B

Correct answer: The officer explained the rules clearly to the visitors.

Explanation: “Clearly” is an adverb describing how the officer explained the rules.

Quick lesson: Use an adverb to describe an action: explain clearly.

Question 60 C

Correct answer: The students, who were late, missed the instructions.

Explanation: “Who” refers to “students,” a plural noun, so the verb is “were.”

Quick lesson: Use who for people; make the relative-clause verb agree with its antecedent.

Question 61 A

Correct answer: She is responsible for organizing the event.

Explanation: The correct expression is “responsible for,” followed by a noun or gerund.

Quick lesson: Use responsible for + noun/verb-ing.

Question 62 A

Correct answer: The manager asked me why I was late.

Explanation: This is an indirect question, so it uses statement word order: subject before verb.

Quick lesson: Indirect question: question word + subject + verb.

Question 63 C

Correct answer: The committee has reached its decision.

Explanation: The sentence treats “committee” as one body and uses the singular possessive “its,” so “has” is the matching verb.

Quick lesson: Collective nouns can be singular when the group acts as one unit.

Question 64 B

Correct answer: The child was so tired that he could not walk.

Explanation: The correct result pattern is “so + adjective + that + clause.”

Quick lesson: Use so + adjective + that; use such + noun phrase + that.

Question 65 C

Correct answer: Neither the manager nor the employees are available.

Explanation: With “neither ... nor,” the verb commonly agrees with the nearer subject. “Employees” is plural.

Quick lesson: Check the subject nearest the verb in neither/nor constructions.

Question 66 A

Correct answer: She asked me if I could help her.

Explanation: An indirect yes/no question uses “if” followed by statement word order.

Quick lesson: Use if/whether + subject + verb in indirect questions.

Question 67 C

Correct answer: The students enjoy participating in school activities.

Explanation: “Students” is plural, so use “enjoy.” The verb “enjoy” is followed by a gerund.

Quick lesson: Use enjoy + verb-ing.

Question 68 A

Correct answer: The new employee is more careful than his coworkers.

Explanation: “Careful” is a longer adjective, so its comparative form is “more careful.”

Quick lesson: Use more + adjective + than for many adjectives with two or more syllables.

Question 69 A

Correct answer: The documents must be submitted before Friday.

Explanation: A passive construction after the modal “must” uses “be” plus the past participle “submitted.”

Quick lesson: Modal passive: must + be + past participle.

Question 70 C

Correct answer: The teacher suggested that he study every day.

Explanation: In formal standard English, “suggested that” can take the subjunctive base form “study.”

Quick lesson: After suggest that, formal exam English often uses the base verb: study, go, be.

Question 71 B

Correct answer: She speaks English as well as her sister.

Explanation: “Well” is the adverb needed to describe how she speaks. “As ... as” makes the comparison.

Quick lesson: Use as + adverb/adjective + as.

Question 72 C

Correct answer: The number of students has increased this year.

Explanation: The subject is “the number,” which is singular, so it takes “has.”

Quick lesson: Use the number of + plural noun + singular verb.

Question 73 A

Correct answer: He was absent because he was sick.

Explanation: “Because” is followed by a complete clause containing a subject and verb.

Quick lesson: Use because + subject + verb; use because of + noun phrase.

Question 74 D

Correct answer: The woman who called you is my supervisor.

Explanation: “Who” is the subject relative pronoun for a person performing the action “called.”

Quick lesson: Use who as the subject for people; whom as an object.

Question 75 B

Correct answer: After finishing the examination, the candidates left the room.

Explanation: After a preposition such as “after,” use a noun or gerund phrase. “Finishing” is the correct gerund.

Quick lesson: Use after + verb-ing when the same person performs both actions.

Sentence Structure — Questions 76–100

Question 76 B

Correct answer: Each of the participants has received a certificate.

Explanation: “Each” is singular, even when it is followed by “of the participants,” so the verb is “has.”

Quick lesson: Each + of + plural noun takes a singular verb.

Question 77 D

Correct answer: My brother, together with his friends, is visiting us.

Explanation: The main subject is the singular “brother.” The phrase “together with his friends” does not change the subject.

Quick lesson: Phrases such as together with do not change subject-verb agreement.

Question 78 A

Correct answer: If I had known about the meeting, I would have attended.

Explanation: This is a third conditional sentence about an unreal past situation. It uses “if + past perfect” and “would have + past participle.”

Quick lesson: Third conditional: If + had + past participle, would have + past participle.

Question 79 C

Correct answer: The book that I borrowed yesterday is interesting.

Explanation: The relative pronoun “that” already serves as the object of “borrowed.” Adding “it” repeats the object unnecessarily.

Quick lesson: Do not repeat the object after a relative pronoun when it already fills that role.

Question 80 B

Correct answer: She is one of the employees who work hard.

Explanation: “Who” refers to “employees,” so the verb in the relative clause is plural: “work.”

Quick lesson: In one of the + plural noun + who clause, make the relative-clause verb agree with the plural noun.

Question 81 D

Correct answer: Despite the heavy rain, the event continued.

Explanation: “Despite” is followed by a noun phrase, not a clause. “The heavy rain” is a noun phrase.

Quick lesson: Use despite + noun/gerund; use although + subject + verb.

Question 82 C

Correct answer: The supervisor made the employees attend the meeting.

Explanation: The verb “make” takes an object followed by the base form of the next verb without “to.”

Quick lesson: Use make + object + base verb.

Question 83 A

Correct answer: Not only the manager but also the employees were informed.

Explanation: With “not only ... but also,” the verb commonly agrees with the nearer subject. “Employees” is plural.

Quick lesson: For not only ... but also, check the subject nearest the verb.

Question 84 D

Correct answer: The news is surprising to everyone.

Explanation: “News” looks plural but is an uncountable singular noun, so it takes “is.”

Quick lesson: News is singular: the news is.

Question 85 B

Correct answer: He is capable of solving difficult problems.

Explanation: The adjective “capable” is followed by the preposition “of” and a gerund.

Quick lesson: Use capable of + noun/verb-ing.

Question 86 A

Correct answer: The students must finish their assignments before they leave.

Explanation: A modal verb such as “must” is followed by the base form of the verb.

Quick lesson: Use modal + base verb; do not add “to” or -s.

Question 87 C

Correct answer: She has fewer mistakes than her classmate.

Explanation: “Mistakes” is a countable plural noun, so “fewer” is used instead of “less.”

Quick lesson: Use fewer with countable nouns and less with uncountable nouns.

Question 88 D

Correct answer: The police are investigating the incident.

Explanation: “Police” is treated as a plural noun in standard English, so it takes “are.”

Quick lesson: Use a plural verb with police: the police are.

Question 89 B

Correct answer: I prefer reading books to watching television.

Explanation: The standard comparison pattern is “prefer X to Y.” The two activities are both gerunds.

Quick lesson: Use prefer + noun/gerund + to + noun/gerund.

Question 90 C

Correct answer: The reason he resigned was that he found another job.

Explanation: The structure “The reason ... was that ...” clearly introduces the explanation without the redundant “reason why ... is because” pattern.

Quick lesson: Use the reason ... is/was that + clause.

Question 91 A

Correct answer: By the time we arrived, the program had already started.

Explanation: The program started before we arrived, so the earlier past action takes the past perfect “had started.”

Quick lesson: Use past perfect for an action completed before another past action.

Question 92 D

Correct answer: The woman who lives next door is a doctor.

Explanation: “Who” is the subject relative pronoun for a person. The verb “lives” agrees with “woman.”

Quick lesson: Use who for a person acting as the subject of a relative clause.

Question 93 B

Correct answer: Unless you study, you will fail the examination.

Explanation: “Unless” means “if not,” so adding “not” after it creates an unnecessary double negative.

Quick lesson: Unless = if not; do not add another not.

Question 94 C

Correct answer: The information provided by the officers was helpful.

Explanation: “Information” is uncountable and singular. The phrase “provided by the officers” only describes it.

Quick lesson: Uncountable nouns such as information take singular verbs.

Question 95 A

Correct answer: She asked whether the documents had been submitted.

Explanation: An indirect yes/no question uses statement word order. “Had been submitted” is the correct past perfect passive form.

Quick lesson: Indirect question: whether + subject + verb; passive past perfect = had been + past participle.

Question 96 D

Correct answer: The manager, as well as the employees, was present.

Explanation: The main subject is the singular “manager.” The phrase “as well as the employees” does not make it plural.

Quick lesson: Ignore interrupting phrases such as as well as when choosing the verb.

Question 97 B

Correct answer: He avoids making unnecessary mistakes.

Explanation: The verb “avoid” is followed by a gerund, not an infinitive.

Quick lesson: Use avoid + verb-ing.

Question 98 A

Correct answer: The project was completed more quickly than we expected.

Explanation: “Quickly” is an adverb, and its comparative form is “more quickly.”

Quick lesson: Use more + adverb for many adverbs ending in -ly.

Question 99 C

Correct answer: There are many reasons to support the proposal.

Explanation: The real subject is the plural noun “reasons,” so the verb is “are.”

Quick lesson: In there is/are sentences, make the verb agree with the noun that follows.

Question 100 D

Correct answer: Had I known about the changes, I would have prepared earlier.

Explanation: This is an inverted third conditional. “Had I known” means “If I had known.”

Quick lesson: In formal conditional inversion, Had + subject + past participle replaces If + subject + had + past participle.

Paragraph Organization — Questions 1–25

Paragraph Organization — Question 1 A

Correct order: 2–3–4–1

Explanation: The teacher first explains the requirements. The students then gather materials, work together, and finally submit their projects.

Quick lesson: Look for sequence words such as first, after that, then, and finally.

Paragraph Organization — Question 2 B

Correct order: 2–3–1–4

Explanation: The heavy rain causes roads to flood. As a result, travel becomes difficult, and authorities advise residents to avoid affected areas.

Quick lesson: Arrange cause before effect. Signal phrases such as as a result usually introduce a consequence.

Paragraph Organization — Question 3 B

Correct order: 2–4–1–3

Explanation: The paragraph introduces exercise, gives two health benefits, and ends with a recommendation.

Quick lesson: Start with the main idea, add supporting details, and finish with the conclusion.

Paragraph Organization — Question 4 C

Correct order: 2–4–3–1

Explanation: Maria begins writing, checks the first draft, corrects errors, and finally saves and prints the report.

Quick lesson: Follow the natural order of completing a task.

Paragraph Organization — Question 5 A

Correct order: 2–3–4–1

Explanation: The paragraph first explains two benefits of forests, then contrasts them with the threat of deforestation, and concludes that forests must be protected.

Quick lesson: A contrast word such as however often introduces a different or opposing idea.

Paragraph Organization — Question 6 C

Correct order: 2–3–1–4

Explanation: People submit applications. Human resources reviews them and selects candidates. The company interviews the qualified applicants and offers the job.

Quick lesson: Track the steps in a hiring process from application to job offer.

Paragraph Organization — Question 7 B

Correct order: 2–3–1–4

Explanation: The paragraph introduces daily waste, explains segregation, describes the problem caused by poor waste management, and ends with a recommendation.

Quick lesson: Connect each sentence to the same topic and use therefore to signal a conclusion.

Paragraph Organization — Question 8 A

Correct order: 2–3–4–1

Explanation: Passengers check in, go through security, wait at the boarding gate, and finally board the aircraft.

Quick lesson: Use the real-world order of airport procedures.

Paragraph Organization — Question 9 B

Correct order: 2–3–4–1

Explanation: The paragraph introduces budgeting, explains that it tracks income and spending, shows how people can find savings, and explains the benefit of better decisions.

Quick lesson: Pronouns such as it and this should follow the idea they refer to.

Paragraph Organization — Question 10 C

Correct order: 2–3–4–1

Explanation: The clean-up activity happens first. Participants collect trash, plant flowers, and the school grounds become cleaner as a result.

Quick lesson: An activity should come before the actions performed during it and the result.

Paragraph Organization — Question 11 B

Correct order: 2–1–3–4

Explanation: The patient visits the clinic, the doctor examines the patient, medicine and advice are given, and the patient later feels better.

Quick lesson: Arrange events in time order: visit, examination, treatment, recovery.

Paragraph Organization — Question 12 C

Correct order: 2–3–4–1

Explanation: The paragraph introduces educational technology, gives examples of online resources and virtual classes, and ends with the result for students.

Quick lesson: A general statement is often followed by examples before the concluding result.

Paragraph Organization — Question 13 A

Correct order: 2–4–1–3

Explanation: The group decides what to prepare, washes and cuts the vegetables, mixes the ingredients, and finally serves the salad.

Quick lesson: Sequence words first, afterward, and finally show the order of steps.

Paragraph Organization — Question 14 B

Correct order: 2–3–1–4

Explanation: The paragraph introduces the harm caused by smoking, gives examples of damage, explains the possible health consequences, and ends with advice to avoid smoking.

Quick lesson: Move from the general issue to specific effects and then to a conclusion.

Paragraph Organization — Question 15 C

Correct order: 2–1–3–4

Explanation: The team identifies the cause, discusses possible solutions, chooses the most practical one, and implements it.

Quick lesson: Problem-solving paragraphs usually move from identifying a problem to choosing and applying a solution.

Paragraph Organization — Question 16 B

Correct order: 2–3–4–1

Explanation: Water is collected, filtered, disinfected, and finally tested and treated for distribution.

Quick lesson: Order process steps from the first stage to the final stage.

Paragraph Organization — Question 17 B

Correct order: 2–3–1–4

Explanation: The paragraph introduces reading, explains that it exposes students to ideas, adds other benefits, and recommends reading daily.

Quick lesson: Use the topic sentence first, supporting benefits next, and a recommendation last.

Paragraph Organization — Question 18 D

Correct order: 2–1–3–4

Explanation: The warning comes first, residents move to safety, emergency teams prepare shelters, and the local government continues monitoring conditions.

Quick lesson: Follow the immediate response and then the continuing emergency actions.

Paragraph Organization — Question 19 A

Correct order: 2–3–4–1

Explanation: Students choose a topic, gather information, organize it into a presentation, and finally present their findings.

Quick lesson: Research projects generally move from topic selection to research, preparation, and presentation.

Paragraph Organization — Question 20 C

Correct order: 2–3–4–1

Explanation: The paragraph introduces handwashing, gives situations when people should wash, explains how soap and water help, and concludes with the health benefit.

Quick lesson: Put the main idea first, then practical details, and finish with the result.

Paragraph Organization — Question 21 C

Correct order: 2–1–3–4

Explanation: The family first checks that the vehicle is ready. The driver inspects it, they pack after the inspection, and then begin the trip.

Quick lesson: A broad introductory sentence can be followed by the specific preparation steps.

Paragraph Organization — Question 22 B

Correct order: 2–3–4–1

Explanation: The store decides to improve service, employees greet customers and respond to concerns, products are arranged properly, and the business attracts more customers.

Quick lesson: Keep supporting actions together before stating their result.

Paragraph Organization — Question 23 C

Correct order: 2–3–4–1

Explanation: He arrives without reviewing instructions, becomes confused, decides to prepare better next time, and learns the importance of preparation.

Quick lesson: Keep the events in time order and place the lesson learned at the end.

Paragraph Organization — Question 24 B

Correct order: 2–3–4–1

Explanation: Farmers prepare the soil, plant and water seeds, wait for the plants to grow, and then harvest crops for use or sale.

Quick lesson: A process paragraph should follow the steps in the order they happen.

Paragraph Organization — Question 25 C

Correct order: 2–3–4–1

Explanation: The paragraph introduces the internet as an information source, warns that some information is unreliable, advises checking sources, and concludes that responsible use matters.

Quick lesson: A contrast word such as however introduces a warning; advice can then lead to the conclusion.

Paragraph Organization — Questions 26–50

Paragraph Organization — Question 26 A

Correct order: 2–3–4–1

Explanation: The farmers prepare the land, care for the growing crops, the plants produce vegetables, and the harvested vegetables are delivered to the market.

Sentence Completion — Question 1 B

Correct answer: B

Explanation: Because explains why the employees continued working: the approaching deadline gave them a reason to keep going. However, the sentence is somewhat awkward because the opening phrase already gives a contrast.

Quick lesson: Use because to introduce a reason. Although introduces a contrast.

Sentence Completion — Question 2 B

Correct answer: B

Explanation: The committee postponed the meeting because several members could not attend. The clause gives the reason for the postponement.

Quick lesson: Because is followed by a complete clause that explains why something happened.

Sentence Completion — Question 3 C

Correct answer: C

Explanation: With neither...nor, the verb usually agrees with the nearer subject. Employees is plural, so were is correct.

Quick lesson: For neither...nor, check the subject nearest to the verb.

Sentence Completion — Question 4 D

Correct answer: D

Explanation: The correct paired structure is not because..., but because.... It contrasts the actual reason with the rejected reason.

Quick lesson: Use not because X, but because Y to correct or contrast a reason.

Sentence Completion — Question 5 A

Correct answer: A

Explanation: So that introduces the purpose of the regulations: ensuring responsible use of public funds.

Quick lesson: Use so that to introduce a purpose or intended result.

Sentence Completion — Question 6 B

Correct answer: B

Explanation: The sentence describes an unreal past condition and its past result. Had read is the past perfect form needed in the if-clause.

Quick lesson: Third conditional: if + past perfect, would/could have + past participle.

Sentence Completion — Question 7 C

Correct answer: C

Explanation: After request that, formal English uses the base form of the verb. Follow is the subjunctive form.

Quick lesson: After verbs such as request, recommend, or insist, use that + subject + base verb.

Error Recognition — Question 53 E

Explanation: Learning and improving are parallel gerunds after the preposition in.

Quick lesson: After a preposition, use a noun or gerund; keep paired forms parallel.

Error Recognition — Question 54 C

Correct answer: C

Explanation: Advice is uncountable; say some advice or a piece of advice, not an advice.

Quick lesson: Do not use a/an directly before uncountable nouns such as advice.

Error Recognition — Question 55 B

Correct answer: B

Explanation: Neither is singular in formal grammar, so use has arrived.

Quick lesson: Neither of + plural noun generally takes a singular verb in formal exam grammar.

Error Recognition — Question 56 E

Correct answer: E

Explanation: Received agrees with the finished past-time expression last week.

Quick lesson: Use simple past with a definite finished past-time expression.

Error Recognition — Question 57 C

Correct answer: C

Explanation: An embedded question uses statement order: where I put, not where did I put.

Quick lesson: In indirect questions, use subject + verb order.

Error Recognition — Question 58 E

Correct answer: E

Explanation: Maintaining and keeping are parallel gerunds after for.

Quick lesson: After a preposition, use a gerund; keep coordinated forms parallel.

Error Recognition — Question 59 B

Correct answer: B

Explanation: Committee members is plural, so use have agreed.

Quick lesson: Plural subjects take have in the present perfect.

Error Recognition — Question 60 C

Correct answer: C

Explanation: The past form is fell: that he fell asleep.

Quick lesson: Use simple past for an action during a past event.

Error Recognition — Question 61 E

Correct answer: E

Explanation: The sentence is grammatically correct; received is in the past tense.

Quick lesson: Check the whole sentence; some sentences have no error.

Error Recognition — Question 62 E

Correct answer: E

Explanation: Encouraged the students to participate is a correct verb pattern.

Quick lesson: Use encourage + object + to-infinitive.

Error Recognition — Question 63 B

Correct answer: B

Explanation: Information is uncountable; use much useful information or many useful facts.

Quick lesson: Information has no plural -s in standard English.

Error Recognition — Question 64 E

Correct answer: E

Explanation: Spoke politely and answered are correctly formed.

Quick lesson: Use an adverb such as politely to describe how someone speaks.

Error Recognition — Question 65 E

Correct answer: E

Explanation: Were instructed agrees with the plural subject students.

Quick lesson: A plural subject takes were in the past passive.

Fractions — Questions 1–50

Fractions — Question 1 B

Correct answer: B

Explanation: 3/4 = 6/8; 6/8 + 1/8 = 7/8.

Quick lesson: Use equivalent fractions with the same denominator before adding.

Fractions — Question 2 C

Correct answer: C

Explanation: The LCD is 12: 5/6 = 10/12 and 1/4 = 3/12. The difference is 7/12.

Quick lesson: For subtraction, convert both fractions to the least common denominator.

Fractions — Question 3 A

Correct answer: A

Explanation: 2×9/(3×10) = 18/30 = 3/5.

Quick lesson: Multiply numerators together and denominators together, then simplify.

Fractions — Question 4 D

Correct answer: D

Explanation: 7/8 × 15/14 = 105/112 = 15/16.

Quick lesson: To divide fractions, keep the first fraction and multiply by the reciprocal of the second.

Fractions — Question 5 B

Correct answer: B

Explanation: The greatest common factor of 42 and 56 is 14. Dividing both by 14 gives 3/4.

Quick lesson: Simplify a fraction by dividing its numerator and denominator by the same common factor.

Fractions — Question 6 C

Correct answer: C

Explanation: 250 × 3/5 = 750/5 = 150.

Quick lesson: To find a fraction of a number, multiply the number by the fraction.

Fractions — Question 7 B

Correct answer: B

Explanation: 2 1/3 = 28/12 and 1 3/4 = 21/12. Their sum is 49/12 = 4 1/12.

Quick lesson: Convert mixed numbers to improper fractions before adding.

Fractions — Question 8 D

Correct answer: D

Explanation: 5 1/2 = 11/2 = 33/6; 2 2/3 = 16/6. Difference: 17/6 = 2 5/6.

Quick lesson: Use a common denominator when subtracting mixed numbers.

Fractions — Question 9 B

Correct answer: B

Explanation: 2/3 = 6/9; 4/9 + 6/9 = 10/9 = 1 1/9.

Quick lesson: Convert to a common denominator, then change an improper fraction to a mixed number.

Fractions — Question 10 C

Correct answer: C

Explanation: 2/5 = 4/10, so 7/10 − 4/10 = 3/10.

Quick lesson: Rewrite fractions with a common denominator before subtracting.

Fractions — Question 11 A

Correct answer: A

Explanation: (3×2)/(4×5) = 6/20 = 3/10.

Quick lesson: Multiply straight across, then reduce the result.

Fractions — Question 12 C

Correct answer: C

Explanation: 5/8 ÷ 1/4 = 5/8 × 4/1 = 20/8 = 2 1/2.

Quick lesson: Divide by a fraction by multiplying by its reciprocal.

Fractions — Question 13 C

Correct answer: C

Explanation: Using denominator 6: 1/3 + 1/6 + 1/2 = 2/6 + 1/6 + 3/6 = 6/6 = 1.

Quick lesson: Find one common denominator when adding several fractions.

Fractions — Question 14 C

Correct answer: C

Explanation: 350 × 2/7 = 700/7 = 100 young trees.

Quick lesson: Multiply the whole amount by the given fraction.

Fractions — Question 15 B

Correct answer: B

Explanation: 96 × 3/8 = 12 × 3 = 36 questions.

Quick lesson: Divide by the denominator first, then multiply by the numerator.

Fractions — Question 16 A

Correct answer: A

Explanation: 4/5 ÷ 2/3 = 4/5 × 3/2 = 12/10 = 6/5.

Quick lesson: Flip only the divisor, then multiply and simplify.

Fractions — Question 17 B

Correct answer: B

Explanation: 3/4 = 6/8, which is greater than 5/8.

Quick lesson: With equal denominators, the fraction with the larger numerator is greater.

Fractions — Question 18 C

Numerical Ability — Decimals Questions 1–50

Verified answers, step-by-step explanations, and quick lessons for Decimal Batch 1.

Decimals — Question 1 C

Correct answer: C. 21.15

Explanation: 12.45 + 8.70 = 21.15.

Quick lesson: Align the decimal points before adding.

Decimals — Question 2 B

Correct answer: B. 18.15

Explanation: 35.80 − 17.65 = 18.15.

Quick lesson: Align decimal points and add a zero when needed.

Correct answer: C. 10.8

Explanation: 4.5 × 2.4 = 10.80 = 10.8.

Quick lesson: Multiply first, then count the total decimal places in the factors.

Explanation: 18.9 ÷ 3 = 6.3.

Quick lesson: Divide as usual; place the decimal point in the quotient.

Correct answer: B. 0.875

Explanation: 0.750 + 0.125 = 0.875.

Quick lesson: Write equal decimal places by adding zeros, then add.

Decimals — Question 6 C

Correct answer: C. 7.8

Explanation: 15.6 × 0.5 = 7.8; multiplying by 0.5 means taking half.

Quick lesson: Multiplying a number by 0.5 gives half of that number.

Correct answer: C. 12

Explanation: 7.2 ÷ 0.6 = 72 ÷ 6 = 12.

Quick lesson: Move both decimal points one place to the right so the divisor becomes a whole number.

Explanation: ₱18.75 × 4 = ₱75.00.

Quick lesson: Multiply the price of one item by the number of items.

Explanation: The hundredths digit is 8 and the next digit is 6, so round 47.386 up to 47.39.

Quick lesson: When the next digit is 5–9, increase the rounding digit by one.

Explanation: Write the numbers with the same number of decimal places: 0.7050, 0.7500, 0.0750, and 0.7055. Comparing from left to right, 0.7500 is the greatest.

Quick lesson: Add zeros to compare decimals, then compare digits from left to right.

Numerical Ability — Percentages Questions 1–25

Percentages — Question 1 C

Correct answer: C. 90

Explanation: 25% = 1/4. Then 360 ÷ 4 = 90.

Quick lesson: To find 25% of a number, divide the number by 4.

Percentages — Question 2 A

Correct answer: A. 90

Explanation: 18% = 0.18. Then 500 × 0.18 = 90.

Quick lesson: Convert a percentage to a decimal by dividing by 100, then multiply.

Percentages — Question 3 D

Correct answer: D. 70

Explanation: 35% = 0.35. Then 200 × 0.35 = 70.

Quick lesson: Multiply the whole number by the percentage written as a decimal.

Percentages — Question 4 B

Correct answer: B. 90

Explanation: 12% = 0.12. Then 750 × 0.12 = 90.

Quick lesson: Convert the percent to a decimal, then multiply by the base number.

Percentages — Question 5 C

Correct answer: C. 100

Explanation: 8% = 0.08. Then 1,250 × 0.08 = 100.

Quick lesson: To find a percentage of a number, multiply by its decimal form.

Percentages — Question 6 A

Correct answer: A. 34

Explanation: 40% = 0.40. Then 85 × 0.40 = 34.

Quick lesson: A percentage means a part out of 100.

Percentages — Question 7 D

Correct answer: D. 20

Explanation: 2.5% = 0.025. Then 800 × 0.025 = 20.

Quick lesson: Move the decimal point two places left to convert a percent to a decimal.

Percentages — Question 8 B

Correct answer: B. 270

Explanation: 60% = 0.60. Then 450 × 0.60 = 270.

Quick lesson: Convert the percentage to a decimal before multiplying.

Percentages — Question 9 C

Correct answer: C. 96

Explanation: 15% of 640 = 10% of 640 (64) + 5% of 640 (32) = 96.

Quick lesson: You can split percentages into easier parts, such as 10% plus 5%.

Percentages — Question 10 A

Correct answer: A. 100

Explanation: 125% = 1.25. Then 80 × 1.25 = 100.

Quick lesson: A percentage greater than 100% gives a result greater than the original number.

Quick lesson: Sale price = original price − discount amount.

Quick lesson: New price after an increase = original price + increase amount.

Correct answer: C. 240

Explanation: Let the number be x. 0.30x = 72, so x = 72 ÷ 0.30 = 240.

Quick lesson: To find the whole when a percentage is known, divide the part by the percentage in decimal form.

Quick lesson: To find the original whole, divide the known part by the percentage as a decimal.

Explanation: A 25% discount means 75% of the original price is paid. ₱2,400 × 0.75 = ₱1,800.

Quick lesson: Amount paid = original price × (1 − discount rate).

Percentages — Question 84 B

Correct answer: B. 25%

Explanation: The increase is 400 − 320 = 80. Percentage increase = 80 ÷ 320 × 100 = 25%.

Quick lesson: Always divide the increase by the original value, not the new value.

Percentages — Question 85 C

Correct answer: C. 280

Explanation: If 75% is 525, the number is 525 ÷ 0.75 = 700. Then 40% of 700 = 280.

Quick lesson: Find the original whole before calculating another percentage of it.

Percentages — Question 86 C

Correct answer: C. ₱1,800

Explanation: After a 20% discount, 80% remains. Original price = ₱1,440 ÷ 0.80 = ₱1,800.

Quick lesson: Original price = sale price ÷ percentage remaining.

Percentages — Question 87 C

Correct answer: C. 40

Explanation: 0.8% = 0.008. Then 5,000 × 0.008 = 40.

Quick lesson: A small percentage must also be converted to decimal form by dividing by 100.

Percentages — Question 88 B

Correct answer: B. 54,000

Explanation: 10% of 60,000 = 6,000. Subtract the decrease: 60,000 − 6,000 = 54,000.

Quick lesson: New population after a decrease = original population − decrease.

Percentages — Question 89 C

Correct answer: C. ₱7,150

Explanation: 22% = 0.22. Then ₱32,500 × 0.22 = ₱7,150.

Quick lesson: Savings = salary × savings rate.

Percentages — Question 90 C

Correct answer: C. ₱1,320

Explanation: First increase: ₱1,000 × 1.20 = ₱1,200. Increase that new price by 10%: ₱1,200 × 1.10 = ₱1,320.

Quick lesson: For successive increases, calculate each increase using the current amount.

Percentages — Question 91 C

Correct answer: C. 40

Explanation: Correct answers = 84% of 250 = 210. Incorrect answers = 250 − 210 = 40.

Quick lesson: Find the number correct, then subtract it from the total.

Percentages — Question 92 B

Correct answer: B. 32.5%

Explanation: 208 ÷ 640 = 0.325. Then 0.325 × 100 = 32.5%.

Quick lesson: Percentage = (part ÷ whole) × 100.

Percentages — Question 93 C

Correct answer: C. ₱1,360

Explanation: A 15% loss means 85% of the cost remains. ₱1,600 × 0.85 = ₱1,360.

Quick lesson: Selling price after a loss = cost price × (1 − loss rate).

Percentages — Question 94 C

Correct answer: C. 800

Explanation: After a 30% decrease, 70% remains. Let the original number be x: 0.70x = 560, so x = 560 ÷ 0.70 = 800.

Quick lesson: Original value = decreased value ÷ percentage remaining.

Quick lesson: Remaining income percentage = 100% − spending percentage.

Quick lesson: Simplify a ratio by dividing every term by the greatest common factor.

Correct answer: C. ₱840

Explanation: One kilogram costs ₱490 ÷ 7 = ₱70. Twelve kilograms cost 12 × ₱70 = ₱840.

Quick lesson: Find the unit cost and multiply by the requested quantity.

Ratio and Proportion — Question 95 B

Correct answer: B. 10 years

Explanation: There are 4 + 1 = 5 parts. One part is 50 ÷ 5 = 10 years. The son represents 1 part, so he is 10 years old.

Quick lesson: For a combined-age ratio, divide the total age by the sum of the ratio terms.

Ratio and Proportion — Question 96 B

Correct answer: B. 12 days

Explanation: The wall requires 8 × 21 = 168 worker-days. With 14 workers, time = 168 ÷ 14 = 12 days.

Quick lesson: For the same work rate and task, workers × days remains constant.

Ratio and Proportion — Question 97 C

Correct answer: C. 480 km

Explanation: The vehicle's speed is 300 ÷ 5 = 60 km/h. In 8 hours, it travels 60 × 8 = 480 km.

Quick lesson: Distance = speed × time.

Ratio and Proportion — Question 98 C

Correct answer: C. 220 girls

Explanation: Nine parts represent 180 boys, so one part is 180 ÷ 9 = 20. Girls = 11 × 20 = 220.

Quick lesson: Use the known group to find one part, then multiply by the other group's ratio term.

Ratio and Proportion — Question 99 B

Correct answer: B. ₱1,500

Explanation: There are 5 + 7 = 12 parts. One part is ₱3,600 ÷ 12 = ₱300. The smaller share is 5 × ₱300 = ₱1,500.

Quick lesson: Choose the smaller ratio term when calculating the smaller share.

Explanation: The job requires 15 × 12 = 180 worker-days. To finish in 9 days, workers needed = 180 ÷ 9 = 20.

Quick lesson: For a fixed amount of work, divide total worker-days by the desired completion time.

Quick lesson: Find the remaining group's sum, then divide by its number of values.

Averages — Question 96 B

Correct answer: B. 31 years

Explanation: The eight workers' total age is 8 × 30 = 240 years. Add the new worker's age: 240 + 39 = 279. New average = 279 ÷ 9 = 31 years.

Quick lesson: When one person joins, add their value and increase the count by one.

Averages — Question 97 B

Correct answer: B. 89

Explanation: The first five subjects total 5 × 88 = 440. Add 94 to get 534. New average = 534 ÷ 6 = 89.

Quick lesson: Add the new score to the old total, then divide by the new number of subjects.

Averages — Question 98 C

Correct answer: C. 39.2

Explanation: The recorded total is 25 × 40 = 1,000. The number 50 was recorded as 70, making the total 20 too high. Correct total = 1,000 − 20 = 980. Correct average = 980 ÷ 25 = 39.2.

Quick lesson: Correct the total by removing the recording error, then divide by the unchanged count.

Averages — Question 99 C

Correct answer: C. 66.67 km/h (approximately)

Explanation: The total distance is 100 + 100 = 200 km. The travel times are 100 ÷ 50 = 2 hours and 100 ÷ 100 = 1 hour, for 3 hours total. Average speed = 200 ÷ 3 = 66.666..., approximately 66.67 km/h.

Quick lesson: Average speed is total distance divided by total time; do not simply average the two speeds.

Averages — Question 100 B

Correct answer: B. 83

Explanation: The original total score is 30 × 82 = 2,460. Correcting 60 to 90 increases the total by 30, giving 2,490. New average = 2,490 ÷ 30 = 83.

Quick lesson: When a score is corrected, adjust the total by the difference and divide by the same number of students.

Algebra — Question 1 B

Simple Interest — Questions 1–50

Verified calculations, explanations, and quick lessons for Simple Interest Batch 1. Questions 27 and 36 have no matching correct option in the question choices and are flagged for correction.

Simple Interest — Question 1 B

Correct answer: B. ₱300

Explanation: I = 2,000 × 5% × 3 = ₱300.

Quick lesson: Multiply the principal by the annual rate and the number of years.

Simple Interest — Question 2 D

Correct answer: D. ₱400

Explanation: I = 5,000 × 4% × 2 = ₱400.

Quick lesson: Convert the rate to a decimal, then multiply by principal and time.

Simple Interest — Question 3 A

Correct answer: A. ₱60

Explanation: Six months is 0.5 year. I = 1,200 × 10% × 0.5 = ₱60.

Quick lesson: Convert months to years by dividing by 12.

Simple Interest — Question 4 C

Correct answer: C. 4%

Explanation: R = (I ÷ (P × T)) × 100 = (240 ÷ (2,000 × 3)) × 100 = 4%.

Quick lesson: To find the rate, divide interest by principal times time, then multiply by 100.

Simple Interest — Question 5 A

Correct answer: A. ₱1,320

Explanation: I = 1,200 × 5% × 2 = ₱120. Amount = 1,200 + 120 = ₱1,320.

Quick lesson: Total amount equals the principal plus the interest.

Correct answer: D. 2 years

Explanation: T = I ÷ (P × R) = 960 ÷ (8,000 × 0.06) = 2 years.

Quick lesson: To find time, divide the interest by principal times the annual rate.

Quick lesson: Calculate total earnings first, then multiply by the percentage saved.

Mixed Word Problems — Question 71 A

Correct answer: A. ₱5,000

Explanation: Cost = 80 × ₱500 = ₱40,000. Revenue = (60 × ₱600) + (20 × ₱450) = ₱45,000. Profit = ₱5,000.

Quick lesson: For mixed selling prices, add the revenue from each group and subtract the total cost.

Mixed Word Problems — Question 72 B

Correct answer: B. 24

Explanation: Original total = 8 × 25 = 200. Remove 32: 200 − 32 = 168. New average = 168 ÷ 7 = 24.

Quick lesson: Find the original total using average × count, adjust the total, then divide by the new count.

Mixed Word Problems — Question 73 C

Correct answer: C. ₱24,000

Explanation: Total expenses = ₱9,000 + ₱6,000 + ₱3,000 = ₱18,000. This is 75% of income. Income = ₱18,000 ÷ 0.75 = ₱24,000.

Quick lesson: When a known amount represents a percentage of a whole, divide the amount by the decimal form of that percentage.

Mixed Word Problems — Question 74 C

Correct answer: C. 1,600 items

Explanation: One machine produces 240 ÷ 3 = 80 items per hour. Five machines in 4 hours produce 80 × 5 × 4 = 1,600 items.

Quick lesson: Multiply the hourly rate per machine by the number of machines and hours.

Mixed Word Problems — Question 75 No valid option

Correct calculation (choices need correction): Correct profit: ₱700 (no matching choice)

Explanation: Revenue = (80 × ₱50) + (20 × ₱35) = ₱4,700. Cost = ₱4,000. Profit = ₱4,700 − ₱4,000 = ₱700. None of the listed choices (₱200, ₱300, ₱400, or ₱500) matches.

Quick lesson: Total profit = total sales revenue − purchase cost. This question's choices need correction; the correct profit is ₱700.

Mixed Word Problems — Question 76 No valid option

Correct calculation (choices need correction): Correct profit: ₱5,250 (no matching choice)

Explanation: Cost = 60 × ₱400 = ₱24,000. Revenue = (45 × ₱500) + (15 × ₱450) = ₱22,500 + ₱6,750 = ₱29,250. Profit = ₱29,250 − ₱24,000 = ₱5,250. None of the listed choices matches.

Quick lesson: Add revenue from both groups, then subtract total purchase cost. This question's choices need correction; the correct profit is ₱5,250.

Mixed Word Problems — Question 77 C

Correct answer: C. ₱8,400

Explanation: Spending = 30% + 25% + 15% = 70%. Remaining = 30% × ₱28,000 = ₱8,400.

Quick lesson: Add spending percentages and subtract from 100% to find the percentage remaining.

Mixed Word Problems — Question 78 C

Correct answer: C. 84 women

Explanation: Total ratio parts = 4 + 7 = 11. One part = 132 ÷ 11 = 12. Women = 7 × 12 = 84.

Quick lesson: Divide the total by the sum of ratio parts, then multiply by the required part.

Mixed Word Problems — Question 79 B

Correct answer: B. 70 km/h

Explanation: Total distance = 210 + 280 = 490 km. Total time = 3 + 4 = 7 hours. Average speed = 490 ÷ 7 = 70 km/h.

Quick lesson: Average speed for the whole journey is total distance divided by total time.

Mixed Word Problems — Question 80 C

Correct answer: C. ₱27,200

Explanation: Discount = 15% × ₱32,000 = ₱4,800. Final price = ₱32,000 − ₱4,800 = ₱27,200.

Quick lesson: After a discount, multiply the original price by 100% minus the discount rate.

Mixed Word Problems — Question 81 B

Correct answer: B. 12 days

Explanation: Total work = 8 × 18 = 144 worker-days. With 12 workers: 144 ÷ 12 = 12 days.

Quick lesson: For the same project, workers × days remains constant.

Mixed Word Problems — Question 82 B

Correct answer: B. 82

Explanation: Required total = 7 × 82 = 574. Six scores total 75 + 80 + 85 + 90 + 78 + 84 = 492. Seventh score = 574 − 492 = 82.

Quick lesson: Find the total needed for the target average, then subtract the known scores.

Mixed Word Problems — Question 83 C

Correct answer: C. 90 kg

Explanation: Sold = 2/5 × 900 = 360 kg. Remaining = 900 − 360 = 540 kg. Each sack gets 540 ÷ 6 = 90 kg.

Quick lesson: Subtract the fraction sold, then divide the remaining amount equally.

Mixed Word Problems — Question 84 C

Correct answer: C. 15%

Explanation: Profit = ₱5,750 − ₱5,000 = ₱750. Profit percentage = 750 ÷ 5,000 × 100 = 15%.

Quick lesson: Profit percentage is based on cost price: profit ÷ cost × 100.

Mixed Word Problems — Question 85 B

Correct answer: B. 12 years

Explanation: Let the son's age be x. Father = x + 36. Then x + (x + 36) = 60, so 2x = 24 and x = 12.

Quick lesson: Translate the age difference and combined age into an equation.

Mixed Word Problems — Question 86 C

Correct answer: C. 2,400 products

Explanation: Production per machine-hour = 900 ÷ (6 × 5) = 30. New production = 30 × 10 × 8 = 2,400 products.

Quick lesson: When both machine count and working hours change, multiply the rate by both new quantities.

Mixed Word Problems — Question 87 B

Correct answer: B. ₱29,000

Explanation: Interest = ₱25,000 × 0.08 × 2 = ₱4,000. Total repayment = ₱25,000 + ₱4,000 = ₱29,000.

Quick lesson: Simple interest = principal × rate × time. Add interest to principal for total repayment.

Mixed Word Problems — Question 88 A

Correct answer: A. 540 boys

Explanation: Boys = 100% − 55% = 45%. Boys = 0.45 × 1,200 = 540.

Quick lesson: Subtract the given percentage from 100% to get the other group's share.

Mixed Word Problems — Question 89 C

Correct answer: C. ₱2,400

Explanation: Fuel efficiency = 360 ÷ 24 = 15 km/L. Fuel for 600 km = 600 ÷ 15 = 40 L. Cost = 40 × ₱60 = ₱2,400.

Quick lesson: Find kilometers per liter, calculate liters needed, then multiply by price per liter.

Mixed Word Problems — Question 90 C

Correct answer: C. 220 items

Explanation: Target total = 180 × 5 = 900. First four days total = 140 + 160 + 180 + 200 = 680. Friday = 900 − 680 = 220.

Quick lesson: Target total = desired average × number of days. Subtract the known totals to find the missing day.

Mixed Word Problems — Question 91 D

Correct answer: D. 15 cups

Explanation: Flour per cake = 6 ÷ 24 = 0.25 cup. For 60 cakes: 0.25 × 60 = 15 cups.

Quick lesson: Use direct proportion to scale ingredients to the new number of servings or items.

Mixed Word Problems — Question 92 C

Correct answer: C. 25%

Explanation: Profit = ₱2,250 − ₱1,800 = ₱450. Profit percentage = 450 ÷ 1,800 × 100 = 25%.

Quick lesson: Profit percentage = (selling price − cost price) ÷ cost price × 100.

Mixed Word Problems — Question 93 C

Correct answer: C. 50%

Explanation: Initial water = 80% × 1,500 = 1,200 L. Remaining = 1,200 − 450 = 750 L. Percentage full = 750 ÷ 1,500 × 100 = 50%.

Quick lesson: Calculate the starting amount, subtract what is used, then divide by capacity to get the remaining percentage.

Mixed Word Problems — Question 94 C

Correct answer: C. 8 hours

Explanation: First leg: 300 ÷ 60 = 5 hours. Second leg: 240 ÷ 80 = 3 hours. Total time = 8 hours.

Quick lesson: Time = distance ÷ speed. Add the times for separate parts of the trip.

Mixed Word Problems — Question 95 B

Correct answer: B. ₱7,875

Explanation: Total earnings = ₱900 × 25 = ₱22,500. Savings = 35% × ₱22,500 = ₱7,875.

Quick lesson: Multiply daily pay by days worked, then calculate the required savings percentage.

Mixed Word Problems — Question 96 B

Correct answer: B. 6:5 (option B: 3:2.5)

Explanation: Male employees = 3/5 × 200 = 120. Female employees = 2/5 × 200 = 80. After 20 more women, female count = 100. New ratio = 120:100 = 6:5 = 3:2.5.

Quick lesson: Convert ratio parts into actual numbers first, add the new employees, then simplify or scale the ratio. Option B (3:2.5) is equivalent to 6:5.

Mixed Word Problems — Question 97 B

Correct answer: B. Correct speed: 72 km/h (no matching choice)

Explanation: Total distance = 150 + 210 = 360 km. Total time = 2 + 3 = 5 hours. Average speed = 360 ÷ 5 = 72 km/h. No listed choice equals 72 km/h.

Quick lesson: Average speed = total distance ÷ total time. The calculated answer is 72 km/h, but none of the choices (65, 70, 75, 80) matches; the choices need correction.

Mixed Word Problems — Question 98 B

Correct answer: B. ₱3,600

Explanation: First discount: ₱5,000 × 0.90 = ₱4,500. Second discount: ₱4,500 × 0.80 = ₱3,600.

Quick lesson: Apply successive discounts one after another, not by simply adding the percentages.

Mixed Word Problems — Question 99 B

Correct answer: B. 33 years

Explanation: Original total age = 5 × 32 = 160. Add the new worker: 160 + 38 = 198. New average = 198 ÷ 6 = 33 years.

Quick lesson: Find the original total, add the new value, and divide by the new number of people.

Mixed Word Problems — Question 100 C

Correct answer: C. 337.5 kg

Explanation: First sale: 40% × 750 = 300 kg. Remaining = 750 − 300 = 450 kg. Second sale = 25% × 450 = 112.5 kg. Left = 450 − 112.5 = 337.5 kg.

Quick lesson: Apply each percentage to the amount remaining at that stage. The second 25% is calculated from 450 kg, not the original 750 kg.

Word Analogy — Question 1 B

Quick lesson: A composer is the creator of a musical composition, just as an author is the creator of a novel.

Word Analogy — Question 94 B
